### Dated law-change ledger (as of August 11, 2026)

| Changeable fact | Current treatment used in this part | Recheck trigger |
|---|---|---|
| IA AUM allocation buffer | Optional SEC registration at $100m–<$110m; existing SEC adviser need not withdraw until below $90m | SEC amendment to Rule 203A-1 or Congress changes §203A |
| Pension-consultant exception | At least $200m of qualifying plan assets under Rule 203A-2 methodology | SEC rule amendment |
| Multi-state exception | 15 or more states | SEC rule amendment or changed state registration footprint |
| Internet-adviser exception | All clients exclusively through an operational interactive website while relying; 2024 amended rule | SEC rule amendment or business-model change |
| Private-fund adviser exemption | Solely private funds; less than $150m U.S. private-fund AUM | Congress/SEC amendment or AUM/client change |
| Qualified-client dollar tests | $1.4m managed or net worth over $2.7m, effective June 29, 2026 | Next SEC inflation order, expected approximately every five years |
| Marketing de minimis compensation | $1,000 over preceding 12 months for specified testimonial/endorsement exceptions | Rule 206(4)-1 amendment |
| Adviser pay-to-play de minimis | $350 if eligible to vote; $150 if not, per official per election | Rule 206(4)-5 amendment |
| Rule 504 ceiling | $10m in 12 months | SEC Regulation D amendment |
| Reg S-P amended compliance | Both larger and smaller entity compliance periods have elapsed by Aug. 2026 | SEC extension/amendment or judicial action |
| Reg S-P customer notice | As soon as practicable, generally ≤30 days after awareness when required | Rule text, exception, or AG delay |
| Reg S-P service-provider control | Notice ASAP and no later than 72 hours after awareness of qualifying breach | Rule text and contract-specific facts |
| Adviser AML/CFT final rule | Effective date postponed to Jan. 1, 2028 as of this part's date | FinCEN review, amendment, withdrawal, or further delay |
| NASAA model rules | Models can change without changing a state's law | State adoption/amendment; always check jurisdiction |

## Closing synthesis

A strong Domain 4 answer normally follows this order:

1. Identify **the actor and capacity**: federal covered IA, state IA, ERA, IAR, BD, agent, issuer agent, underwriter, trustee, solicitor, or client.
2. Ask whether the conduct falls within a **definition**, then test an exclusion and only then a registration exemption.
3. Separate **firm registration**, **individual registration**, **security registration**, and **transaction exemption**.
4. Resolve federal-state allocation and preemption without erasing state notice, IAR, and antifraud power.
5. Apply disclosure, fiduciary, supervision, recordkeeping, and ethical duties even when registration is not required.
6. Identify the Administrator's correct tool and procedural limit.
7. Treat registration, notice filing, exemption, and exam passage as regulatory statuses—not approval, merit findings, guarantees, or licenses beyond their scope.
8. When a number or deadline can change, identify the rule, effective date, and jurisdiction rather than trusting an undated mnemonic.

The recurring legal theme is not “find a loophole.” It is **classify accurately, disclose fully, put the client first, preserve proof, and respect the regulator with jurisdiction**.

## Exact Exam-Language Crosswalk

- **Unlawful representation concerning registration:** saying or implying that filing, effectiveness, exemption, or notice filing means a regulator approved a person, security, transaction, disclosure, or investment merit violates USA §405. “Unlawful registration representation” is a communications offense; it is not cured because the underlying registration is valid.
- **Conflict of interest:** a material incentive or relationship that can consciously or unconsciously incline advice away from the client's best interest. Federal fiduciary law requires full and fair disclosure and informed consent where possible; some conflicts must instead be mitigated, eliminated, or avoided.
- **Pay to play:** Rule 206(4)-5's two-year compensation timeout and contribution/solicitation restrictions are described above. The hyphenated “pay-to-play” and unhyphenated “pay to play” refer to the same subject, but not to every campaign-finance rule.
- **Sharing in profits and losses:** this exam phrase refers to an associated person's participation in the economics of a customer account. Applicable FINRA rules, firm approval, proportional-contribution limits, immediate-family treatment, adviser performance-fee law, guarantees, and fiduciary conflicts must be distinguished.
- **Outside securities account:** an account held away from the employing firm can trigger FINRA Rule 3210 consent, notice, and duplicate-record obligations for associated persons. An adviser access person's **outside securities account** also enters Code of Ethics holdings/account reporting even when FINRA Rule 3210 does not apply.
- **Statute of limitations:** this is the time bar for bringing a claim or prosecution. The tested USA §410 civil rule uses the earlier of three years after the sale/advice or two years after discovery. USA §409's five-year criminal charging sentence is optional model text. Actual jurisdictions can use different limitation or repose periods, tolling, and discovery rules.
- **Private fund adviser:** an adviser to a §3(c)(1) or §3(c)(7) private fund does not receive an automatic exemption merely because the fund is private. It must satisfy SEC registration, §203(l), §203(m), or another route and then analyze state law.
- **Issuer agent:** an individual representing an issuer in effecting or attempting securities sales. USA §401(b) excludes only specified issuer representatives and transactions; transaction-based compensation and nonexempt public solicitation are major warning facts.

# Final Practice Examination A (60 Questions)

## Instructions

This 60-question examination is entirely original. It proportionally emphasizes the four official domains as requested: **9 economics**, **15 investment vehicles**, **18 client recommendations**, and **18 law/ethics**. Allow approximately **77 minutes**. Use no notes. Select the single best answer to each question. Record an answer for every item; detailed answers and rationales follow after the exam marker.

## Questions 1–9: Economic Factors and Business Information

**1.** During an economic contraction, Congress increases infrastructure spending while leaving tax rates unchanged. This action is best described as

A. expansionary fiscal policy
B. restrictive fiscal policy
C. expansionary monetary policy
D. restrictive monetary policy

**2.** A newly issued fixed-rate bond has a 4% coupon when comparable market yields are 4%. One month later, comparable yields rise to 5%, with all other factors unchanged. The bond’s market price will most likely

A. rise above par
B. remain at par
C. fall below par
D. become unrelated to interest rates

**3.** Which transaction is recorded as a cash outflow from investing activities on a corporation’s statement of cash flows?

A. Payment of employee wages
B. Purchase of manufacturing equipment
C. Issuance of common stock
D. Receipt of cash from customers

**4.** A company has current assets of $900,000, inventory of $300,000, and current liabilities of $450,000. Its quick ratio is

A. 0.67
B. 1.00
C. 1.33
D. 2.00

**5.** An investment has a 30% probability of returning 12%, a 50% probability of returning 6%, and a 20% probability of returning −5%. Its expected return is

A. 4.6%
B. 5.6%
C. 6.0%
D. 7.2%

**6.** Which risk can generally be reduced most effectively by holding securities of many unrelated issuers?

A. Market risk
B. Purchasing-power risk
C. Business risk
D. Interest-rate risk

**7.** A portfolio earns a nominal return of 8% while inflation is 3%. Using the exact formula, the real return is closest to

A. 3.0%
B. 4.9%
C. 5.0%
D. 11.0%

**8.** Two portfolios have the same average return and risk-free rate. Portfolio X has a standard deviation of 8%; Portfolio Y has a standard deviation of 12%. Based solely on this information, which statement is most accurate?

A. Portfolio Y must have the higher Sharpe ratio.
B. Portfolio X must have the higher Sharpe ratio.
C. The portfolios must have equal beta.
D. Standard deviation measures only systematic risk.

**9.** In a corporate liquidation, which claimant generally has the lowest priority?

A. Secured bondholders
B. General creditors
C. Preferred shareholders
D. Common shareholders

## Questions 10–24: Investment Vehicle Characteristics

**10.** Which instrument is an unsecured short-term promissory note ordinarily issued by a corporation?

A. Bank certificate of deposit
B. Commercial paper
C. Treasury bill
D. Repurchase agreement

**11.** The principal value of a Treasury Inflation-Protected Security is generally adjusted based on changes in

A. the federal-funds target rate
B. the Consumer Price Index
C. the issuer’s credit rating
D. the prime rate

**12.** A municipal revenue bond is primarily supported by

A. the taxing power of the issuing municipality
B. revenue from a specified facility or project
C. the U.S. government’s full faith and credit
D. assessments against all property in the state

**13.** Two otherwise comparable bonds differ only in modified duration: Bond A has a duration of 3 and Bond B has a duration of 7. If market yields rise 1%, which outcome is most likely?

A. Bond A falls about 7%; Bond B falls about 3%.
B. Bond A rises about 3%; Bond B rises about 7%.
C. Bond A falls about 3%; Bond B falls about 7%.
D. Both bonds fall by exactly the same percentage.

**14.** A convertible corporate bond’s conversion feature generally becomes more valuable when

A. the issuer’s common-stock price rises substantially
B. the issuer eliminates all common shares
C. market volatility disappears
D. the bond’s coupon is paid in cash

**15.** Which right is ordinarily associated with common stock rather than corporate bonds?

A. Priority over preferred stock in liquidation
B. Voting for directors
C. A contractual obligation to receive interest
D. A stated maturity date

**16.** An analyst estimates next year’s dividend at $2 per share, a required return of 10%, and perpetual dividend growth of 4%. Under the constant-growth dividend-discount model, estimated value is closest to

A. $20.00
B. $25.00
C. $33.33
D. $50.00

**17.** In a primary initial public offering, proceeds from the sale of newly issued shares generally go to

A. the issuing company
B. existing shareholders who sell in the secondary market
C. the securities exchange
D. holders of outstanding corporate bonds

**18.** Which statement correctly distinguishes an open-end mutual fund from an exchange-traded fund?

A. An open-end fund normally transacts with investors at a price based on end-of-day NAV; an ETF normally trades intraday on an exchange.
B. An open-end fund always trades at a discount; an ETF is always priced at NAV.
C. An open-end fund has a fixed number of shares; an ETF continuously redeems only for retail investors.
D. Only an open-end fund may hold a diversified portfolio.

**19.** A closed-end fund’s shares trade at $18 while its NAV is $20. The shares trade at

A. a 10% discount to NAV
B. an 11.1% discount to market price
C. a 10% premium to NAV
D. par value

**20.** A Class A mutual-fund shareholder reaches a breakpoint. The breakpoint most directly reduces the

A. investment adviser’s management fee
B. front-end sales charge percentage
C. fund’s portfolio turnover
D. redemption value below NAV

**21.** An investor buys one call option. Which statement is correct regarding the buyer’s position at expiration?

A. Maximum loss is unlimited.
B. Maximum loss is generally the premium paid.
C. Profit is guaranteed if the underlying price falls.
D. The buyer is obligated to sell the underlying asset.

**22.** Compared with a conventional unleveraged index fund held for several months, a daily-reset leveraged ETF presents particular risk because

A. its long-term result can diverge substantially from a simple multiple of the index’s cumulative return
B. it cannot decline when its benchmark rises
C. it is insured against market loss
D. its leverage affects only distributions, not NAV

**23.** Which feature is generally associated with a variable annuity during accumulation?

A. The insurance company guarantees every subaccount’s market return.
B. Earnings grow tax deferred, while subaccount value fluctuates with investment performance.
C. Contributions are always deductible from federal taxable income.
D. Withdrawals are always federally tax free.

**24.** Which statement about digital assets is most appropriate for an investment professional’s product analysis?

A. All digital assets are securities under every set of facts.
B. Digital assets cannot be lost because blockchain records are permanent.
C. The legal status, custody method, liquidity, valuation, technology, fraud, and volatility risks require fact-specific review.
D. Government deposit insurance ordinarily covers all exchange-held digital assets.

## Questions 25–42: Client Investment Recommendations and Strategies

**25.** A client has stable income, a long time horizon, substantial emergency reserves, and says she is emotionally uncomfortable with any loss. Which statement is most accurate?

A. Her risk capacity and risk tolerance must be identical.
B. Her financial risk capacity may be high while her stated risk tolerance is low.
C. Her long time horizon eliminates the need to assess risk tolerance.
D. Her emergency reserves require an all-equity allocation.

**26.** Before recommending an investment strategy to a new individual client, an adviser should ordinarily give greatest initial emphasis to

A. last quarter’s top-performing asset class
B. the adviser’s preferred product lineup
C. the client’s goals, resources, obligations, time horizon, liquidity needs, tax circumstances, knowledge, and risk profile
D. the strategy used by clients of similar age, without gathering more facts

**27.** A client refuses to sell a losing stock because “it is not a loss until I sell,” while readily selling winners. This behavior most directly illustrates

A. loss aversion and the disposition effect
B. diversification
C. dollar-cost averaging
D. duration matching

**28.** Under modern portfolio theory, the principal benefit of combining assets whose returns are not perfectly positively correlated is the potential to

A. eliminate every form of risk
B. reduce portfolio variability without necessarily reducing expected return proportionately
C. guarantee a positive return
D. increase each asset’s stand-alone expected return

**29.** According to CAPM, if the risk-free rate is 3%, the expected market return is 9%, and a security’s beta is 1.5, the security’s required return is

A. 6%
B. 9%
C. 12%
D. 16.5%

**30.** A portfolio policy sets a 60% stock and 40% bond target. After a stock rally, the mix becomes 72% stock and 28% bonds. Rebalancing to policy would generally involve

A. buying stocks and selling bonds
B. selling some stocks and buying bonds
C. selling both asset classes for cash
D. making no trades because allocation targets never require review

**31.** Which statement about dollar-cost averaging is correct?

A. It guarantees a gain if contributions continue long enough.
B. It involves investing a fixed dollar amount at regular intervals, buying more shares at lower prices and fewer at higher prices.
C. It eliminates market risk.
D. It is profitable only when every purchase price increases.

**32.** A client in a 35% marginal federal bracket is comparing a 3.9% tax-exempt municipal yield with taxable alternatives. Ignoring state taxes and other adjustments, the municipal bond’s tax-equivalent yield is

A. 2.54%
B. 3.90%
C. 6.00%
D. 11.14%

**33.** An investor sells stock at a loss and, 12 days later, buys substantially identical stock. Under the federal wash-sale rule, the loss is generally

A. immediately deductible without adjustment
B. permanently ignored with no basis effect
C. disallowed currently and generally added to the replacement shares’ basis
D. converted into ordinary income

**34.** Which statement most accurately compares traditional and Roth IRAs under general federal tax principles?

A. Qualified Roth distributions are generally tax free, while traditional IRA distributions may be taxable.
B. Roth contributions are always deductible; traditional contributions never are.
C. Both always require lifetime distributions from the original owner at the same age.
D. Neither can receive a permissible rollover.

**35.** Which plan is specifically designed to permit an eligible self-employed individual with no employees other than a spouse to maintain a 401(k)-type arrangement?

A. Coverdell ESA
B. Solo 401(k)
C. Section 529 plan
D. UGMA account

**36.** An ERISA plan fiduciary selects an investment because it pays the fiduciary a personal fee, even though a lower-cost comparable choice is available. This most directly raises concern about

A. a prohibited transaction and breach of fiduciary duty
B. a qualified domestic-relations order
C. a permissible settlor function
D. an insured bank deposit

**37.** Which statement about a 529 savings plan is generally correct?

A. The beneficiary always controls withdrawals upon reaching majority.
B. Qualified education withdrawals may receive favorable federal tax treatment, subject to current law.
C. Contributions are always deductible for federal income-tax purposes.
D. The account may invest only in federal government bonds.

**38.** Property registered as joint tenants with right of survivorship ordinarily passes at one owner’s death

A. under that owner’s will only
B. to the surviving joint owner by operation of the ownership form
C. to the state automatically
D. to creditors before any ownership transfer can occur

**39.** A qualified domestic-relations order is most closely associated with

A. directing certain retirement-plan benefits to an alternate payee such as a former spouse or dependent
B. opening a custodial account for a minor
C. registering an investment adviser
D. valuing a charitable deduction

**40.** A client enters an order to buy a stock at $40 or lower. This is a

A. buy-stop order
B. market order
C. buy-limit order
D. sell-stop order

**41.** Which statement best describes time-weighted return?

A. It is especially sensitive to the size and timing of client-controlled cash flows.
B. It links subperiod returns to reduce the effect of external cash-flow timing and is useful for evaluating a manager.
C. It is identical to current yield.
D. It cannot be used when a portfolio has distributions.

**42.** A client invests $10,000 at the beginning of Year 1 and adds $100,000 just before a poor Year 2. Which measure is most likely to reflect the investor’s actual cash-flow experience more directly?

A. Dollar-weighted return
B. Time-weighted return
C. Current yield
D. Price-to-book ratio

## Questions 43–60: Laws, Regulations, and Ethical Practices

**43.** Under the Uniform Securities Act definition, which person is most likely an investment adviser?

A. A bank acting within the statutory bank exclusion
B. A publisher issuing bona fide, impersonal financial commentary of general circulation
C. A person in the business of providing individualized securities advice for compensation
D. An accountant whose securities comments are solely incidental to accounting practice and who receives no special compensation

**44.** A large adviser registered with the SEC conducts advisory business in a state. The state generally may require the adviser to

A. register as a state investment adviser in all cases
B. make a notice filing and pay a fee if permitted by applicable law
C. surrender its SEC registration
D. avoid all state antifraud authority

**45.** Which individual is most likely an investment adviser representative subject to state registration where the individual has a place of business?

A. A clerical employee who only schedules meetings
B. A supervised person who regularly formulates securities advice and meets with clients
C. A janitor employed by an advisory firm
D. An employee whose only function is maintaining computer hardware

**46.** An individual who is not associated with a firm eligible to submit Form U4 wants to take the Series 65. Which statement is most accurate?

A. The exam can never be taken without broker-dealer sponsorship.
B. The individual may generally enroll through FINRA’s TESS process, but passing alone does not create state registration.
C. TESS enrollment automatically registers the person in every state.
D. The individual must first form an investment company.

**47.** Under the Uniform Securities Act, an agent is generally an individual who

A. represents a broker-dealer or issuer in effecting or attempting to effect securities transactions, subject to statutory exceptions
B. gives only impersonal advice through a newspaper
C. audits an issuer’s financial statements
D. administers a state securities division

**48.** Which statement correctly distinguishes an exempt security from an exempt transaction?

A. An exempt security loses antifraud protection; an exempt transaction does not.
B. An exempt security concerns the instrument; an exempt transaction concerns the circumstances of a particular sale, and antifraud provisions may still apply to both.
C. Both terms mean that every person involved is exempt from registration.
D. Only federal law recognizes either concept.

**49.** A state administrator learns that an applicant has ceased doing business and cannot be located after reasonable search. Which administrative action generally differs from a disciplinary revocation because it is used to clean the registration record rather than punish misconduct?

A. Cancellation
B. Criminal conviction
C. Injunction
D. Restitution

**50.** An adviser tells a prospect, “Because I am registered, the state has approved my skill and will reimburse any losses.” This statement is

A. permissible if included in Form ADV
B. an unlawful implication of official approval and an improper guarantee
C. permissible for a federal covered adviser only
D. required whenever performance is discussed

**51.** An advisory contract states that the client waives every right provided by state and federal securities law. The provision is most likely

A. enforceable whenever printed in bold
B. problematic because an adviser generally may not use a hedge clause to mislead a client into believing nonwaivable legal rights were surrendered
C. required in discretionary accounts
D. valid only if the adviser charges a wrap fee

**52.** Before an adviser recommends that a client buy a security from the adviser’s own inventory, the adviser should recognize that the transaction is

A. an agency cross transaction with no conflict
B. a principal transaction requiring appropriate written disclosure and client consent under applicable law
C. always prohibited regardless of disclosure
D. outside the adviser’s fiduciary duty

**53.** Which action best reflects an adviser’s duty to seek best execution?

A. Always selecting the venue with the lowest stated commission, regardless of execution quality
B. Seeking the most favorable total result reasonably available, considering price, costs, speed, likelihood of execution, and other relevant factors
C. Routing every trade to an affiliated broker without disclosure or review
D. Guaranteeing execution at the day’s lowest price

**54.** An adviser has authority to select securities and trade amounts for a client without obtaining consent before each trade. This is most directly

A. discretion, which may trigger written-authority and regulatory requirements
B. custody solely because advice is provided
C. an exempt transaction
D. a guarantee against loss

**55.** An adviser has custody of client funds. Which control is most consistent with custody safeguards?

A. Commingling client funds with the adviser’s operating cash
B. Holding client assets with a qualified custodian and providing required notices, statements, or examinations as applicable
C. Sending statements only when a client complains
D. Using client funds temporarily to pay the adviser’s rent

**56.** An IAR learns that an issuer’s unannounced clinical trial failed and buys put options before the information becomes public. The strongest concern is

A. dollar-cost averaging
B. trading while aware of material nonpublic information
C. tax-loss harvesting
D. strategic asset allocation

**57.** An adviser recommends frequent in-and-out trades primarily to generate transaction-based compensation, with no reasonable client benefit. This is most likely

A. churning or excessive trading
B. passive management
C. a qualified client arrangement
D. a custodial safeguard

**58.** A client offers an adviser permission to borrow $25,000 from the client on favorable terms. The adviser should

A. assume oral consent cures every conflict
B. evaluate applicable prohibitions or narrow exceptions and the fiduciary conflict before accepting; client permission alone may be insufficient
C. accept because borrowing never implicates fiduciary duty
D. conceal the loan from compliance personnel to protect privacy

**59.** An advisory firm discovers unauthorized access to client information. Which response is most appropriate?

A. Ignore the event unless money has already been stolen.
B. Follow the incident-response and business-continuity procedures, contain and investigate the event, preserve evidence, assess notification duties, remediate controls, and document the response.
C. Delete all logs immediately.
D. Publicly disclose every client’s account number.

**60.** An elderly client abruptly requests a large transfer to a new acquaintance and appears confused. The most appropriate initial response is to

A. process the transfer immediately without inquiry because exploitation rules never permit protective action
B. follow applicable vulnerable-adult procedures, escalate internally, document reasonable concerns, and consider authorized trusted-contact or regulator/law-enforcement steps consistent with law
C. accuse the acquaintance publicly of a crime
D. take permanent ownership of the client’s assets for protection


# Detailed Answers and Rationales

## Questions 1–20

**1. A — Expansionary fiscal policy.** Government spending and taxation are fiscal tools; increasing infrastructure spending raises aggregate demand and is expansionary. Monetary policy is conducted through central-bank tools, so C and D misclassify the actor and instrument. Restrictive fiscal policy would reduce spending or increase taxes, contrary to the facts.

**2. C — Fall below par.** A fixed 4% coupon is less attractive when comparable yields rise to 5%, so buyers will pay less until the bond’s yield becomes competitive. A price above par would be associated with a coupon above the prevailing required yield. Existing fixed-rate bond price and market yield generally move inversely.

**3. B — Purchase of manufacturing equipment.** Buying long-lived productive assets is an investing cash outflow. Wages and customer receipts are operating cash flows. Proceeds from issuing stock are financing cash flows.

**4. C — 1.33.** Quick assets exclude inventory: $(900{,}000-300{,}000)/450{,}000=1.333$. Choice D is the current ratio, $900{,}000/450{,}000=2.00$, not the quick ratio. A and B do not follow from the stated amounts.

**5. B — 5.6%.** Expected return is probability weighted: $(0.30)(12\%)+(0.50)(6\%)+(0.20)(-5\%)=3.6\%+3.0\%-1.0\%=5.6\%$. It is not the simple average of the three possible returns because the probabilities differ.

**6. C — Business risk.** Issuer-specific business risk is unsystematic and can generally be reduced through diversification among unrelated issuers. Market, inflation, and broad interest-rate risk are systematic forces that diversification cannot eliminate.

**7. B — Approximately 4.9%.** Exact real return is $(1.08/1.03)-1=0.04854$, or about 4.9%. Choice C is the common approximation $8\%-3\%=5\%$, not the exact result. A and D respectively confuse inflation alone and addition.

**8. B — Portfolio X must have the higher Sharpe ratio.** Sharpe equals excess return divided by total standard deviation. With the same return and risk-free rate, the same numerator divided by 8% is larger than when divided by 12%. Standard deviation includes total variability, not only systematic risk, and it says nothing by itself about the portfolios’ betas.

**9. D — Common shareholders.** In a typical corporate liquidation, secured claims and creditors are paid before equity; preferred shareholders rank ahead of common shareholders. Common equity is the residual claim and therefore generally last.

**10. B — Commercial paper.** Commercial paper is unsecured, short-term corporate debt. A certificate of deposit is a bank obligation, a Treasury bill is a U.S. government obligation, and a repurchase agreement is a collateralized sale-and-repurchase financing arrangement.

**11. B — Consumer Price Index.** TIPS principal is adjusted using an inflation index tied to CPI, subject to the instrument’s terms. The federal-funds rate, prime rate, and issuer credit rating do not mechanically determine the inflation adjustment.

**12. B — Revenue from a specified facility or project.** Revenue bonds depend principally on revenue from the financed enterprise or another pledged stream. General-obligation bonds rely on taxing power. Municipal revenue debt does not carry the U.S. government’s full faith and credit, and a statewide property assessment is not the defining source.

**13. C — Bond A falls about 3%; Bond B falls about 7%.** The duration approximation is $\%\Delta P\approx-D_{mod}\Delta y$. For a one-percentage-point yield rise, the approximate changes are −3% and −7%. The approximation is directionally inverse; B wrongly has prices rising, A swaps durations, and D ignores different sensitivity.

**14. A — The issuer’s common-stock price rises substantially.** The conversion option becomes more valuable as the common stock approaches or exceeds the conversion economics. Paying the coupon does not itself enhance conversion value, and eliminating common shares or assuming no volatility does not support the option’s value.

**15. B — Voting for directors.** Common shareholders ordinarily have voting rights. Bondholders are creditors with contractual payment terms and maturity, while preferred—not common—typically has an equity liquidation preference over common. Common dividends are not contractual interest.

**16. C — $33.33.** The constant-growth model gives $P_0=D_1/(k-g)=2/(0.10-0.04)=2/0.06=\$33.33$. The model requires $k>g$ and uses the next dividend, not an arbitrary multiple.

**17. A — The issuing company.** A primary offering sells newly issued shares and raises issuer capital. A secondary-market sale transfers existing shares between owners; proceeds go to the selling holder, not the issuer. Exchanges and bondholders do not receive the equity issuance proceeds merely by role.

**18. A — Open-end funds transact at end-of-day NAV; ETFs trade intraday.** An open-end fund generally issues and redeems at the next computed NAV (plus any applicable sales charge), while ETF shares trade on an exchange and may move around NAV. Neither structure guarantees a premium or discount, and both can diversify.

**19. A — A 10% discount to NAV.** Discount is $(NAV-market\ price)/NAV=(20-18)/20=10\%$. Measuring against market price produces 11.1%, but fund premium/discount convention uses NAV as the base. Because market price is lower than NAV, this is not a premium.

**20. B — Front-end sales-charge percentage.** Class A breakpoints reduce the sales-load percentage as the qualifying purchase amount rises, subject to rules such as rights of accumulation or letters of intent. They do not directly change portfolio turnover, management fee, or NAV mechanics.

## Questions 21–40

**21. B — Maximum loss is generally the premium paid.** A long call gives the right, not the obligation, to buy; if it expires worthless, the buyer loses the premium. The seller, not buyer, bears potentially very large call risk. A call benefits from an increase, not a decline, in the underlying price.

**22. A — Long-term performance can diverge from a simple multiple.** Daily reset, compounding, volatility, expenses, and path dependency can make a leveraged ETF’s multi-day return differ materially from the stated daily multiple times the index’s cumulative result. It has no insurance against market loss; leverage affects NAV and can amplify downside.

**23. B — Tax deferral with fluctuating subaccounts.** Variable-annuity accumulation values depend on separate-account/subaccount performance, and earnings generally grow tax deferred. The insurer does not guarantee every market subaccount. Contributions are not generally deductible merely because the product is an annuity, and distributions are not automatically tax free.

**24. C — Fact-specific legal, operational, and market review is required.** “Digital asset” covers varied arrangements; classification and risks depend on facts. Blockchain persistence does not prevent key loss, fraud, insolvency, hacking, valuation problems, or illiquidity. Deposit insurance does not ordinarily protect exchange-held digital assets as a class.

**25. B — Capacity may be high while tolerance is low.** Risk capacity is the financial ability to absorb loss; tolerance is the client’s psychological willingness. Stable income, reserves, and time may support capacity, while stated discomfort indicates low tolerance. Neither factor automatically dictates an all-equity allocation or can be ignored.

**26. C — Gather the complete client profile.** A defensible recommendation begins with objectives and constraints, finances, time, liquidity, tax circumstances, knowledge, experience, risk capacity, and tolerance. Recent performance, product availability, or demographic similarity cannot substitute for individualized facts.

**27. A — Loss aversion and disposition effect.** The investor treats realization as psychologically decisive and sells winners while holding losers, a classic disposition pattern linked to loss aversion. Diversification, dollar-cost averaging, and duration matching describe portfolio techniques, not this bias.

**28. B — Potentially reduce variability efficiently.** Combining imperfectly correlated assets can lower portfolio risk because they do not move together perfectly; expected return need not fall proportionally. Diversification cannot guarantee profit or eliminate systematic risk, and it does not alter each asset’s stand-alone expected return.

**29. C — 12%.** CAPM gives $3\%+1.5(9\%-3\%)=3\%+9\%=12\%$. Choice B ignores beta, A gives only the market risk premium, and D multiplies without applying the CAPM structure.

**30. B — Sell some stocks and buy bonds.** Rebalancing restores target weights by trimming the overweight asset and adding to the underweight asset, subject to taxes, costs, and client circumstances. Buying more stock would magnify drift; targets do require periodic monitoring rather than permanent inaction.

**31. B — Fixed sums at regular intervals.** A constant dollar contribution buys more units when price is low and fewer when high. The technique can impose discipline but does not guarantee profit, eliminate market risk, or ensure recovery; an investor must remain able to continue purchases.

**32. C — 6.00%.** Tax-equivalent yield is $3.9\%/(1-0.35)=3.9\%/0.65=6.0\%$. Choice A calculates an after-tax taxable yield instead. B omits the tax adjustment, and D improperly divides by the tax rate.

**33. C — Loss deferred into replacement basis.** A purchase of substantially identical stock within the wash-sale window generally disallows the current loss and adds it to replacement basis, with holding-period adjustments as applicable. The loss is not simply converted to income or deducted immediately.

**34. A — Qualified Roth distributions are generally tax free.** Traditional IRA distributions may be taxable depending on deductible and after-tax basis, while qualified Roth distributions generally receive tax-free treatment. Deductibility is eligibility-dependent, not absolute. The choices also overstate required distributions and rollover restrictions.

**35. B — Solo 401(k).** A one-participant or Solo 401(k) is designed for a self-employed owner with no common-law employees other than a spouse, subject to current rules. A 529 and Coverdell are education arrangements; UGMA is a custodial ownership form.

**36. A — Prohibited transaction and fiduciary breach.** Self-dealing for personal compensation conflicts with ERISA loyalty and prudence duties and can constitute a prohibited transaction absent an applicable exemption. A QDRO allocates benefits; settlor functions concern plan design, and deposit insurance is irrelevant.

**37. B — Qualified withdrawals may be tax favored.** Federal law generally permits tax-free qualified education distributions from 529 plans, subject to current definitions and coordination rules. The account owner ordinarily controls the account, federal deductibility is not universal, and available portfolios are not limited to government bonds.

**38. B — Passes by survivorship.** Joint tenancy with right of survivorship ordinarily transfers a decedent’s interest to the surviving joint tenant outside the will. This differs from tenancy in common, where an interest generally enters the owner’s estate. Creditor rights may matter but do not redefine the survivorship feature.

**39. A — Retirement benefits to an alternate payee.** A QDRO is a domestic-relations order meeting statutory requirements to recognize an alternate payee’s right to qualified-plan benefits. It is not a custodial-account document, adviser registration, or charitable valuation technique.

**40. C — Buy-limit order.** A buy limit sets the maximum acceptable purchase price: $40 or lower. A buy stop normally activates at or above its stop; a market order prioritizes execution over price; a sell stop concerns a sale.

## Questions 41–60

**41. B — Links subperiod returns.** Time weighting geometrically links returns between external cash flows, reducing the impact of client-controlled contribution and withdrawal timing and making it useful for manager evaluation. Dollar-weighted return is cash-flow sensitive. Time weighting is neither current yield nor unusable when distributions occur.

**42. A — Dollar-weighted return.** Dollar-weighted return is the IRR on the investor’s actual cash flows, so the large contribution before a poor period materially affects it. Time-weighted return intentionally neutralizes much of that cash-flow timing effect. Yield and P/B do not measure the investor’s full experience.

**43. C — Individualized securities advice for compensation.** The core adviser definition involves being in the business of advising others about securities for compensation, subject to exclusions. The bank and bona fide impersonal publisher are statutory exclusions; professionals such as accountants may be excluded when advice is solely incidental and no special compensation is received.

**44. B — Notice filing and fee.** A federal covered adviser is generally not dual-registered as a state adviser, but a state may require permitted notice documents and fees and retains important antifraud and investigative authority. SEC registration does not immunize the adviser from all state law.

**45. B — Advice-formulating supervised person.** A supervised person who formulates advice and works with clients is within the functional IAR concept and may require state registration where the person has a place of business, subject to law. Pure clerical, janitorial, and IT-support roles do not ordinarily perform IAR functions merely by working for an adviser.

**46. B — TESS enrollment, not automatic registration.** Series 65 does not require firm sponsorship, and an eligible individual may use FINRA’s TESS route. Exam enrollment and a passing result do not themselves submit or approve a state registration. Form U4 and state procedures remain separate where applicable.

**47. A — Individual representing a broker-dealer or issuer in transactions.** The USA agent definition focuses on an individual who represents a broker-dealer or issuer in effecting or attempting to effect securities transactions, with issuer-agent exceptions. Publishers, auditors, and administrators do not become agents through those functions alone.

**48. B — Instrument versus transaction; antifraud remains.** An exempt security is exempt by the nature/status of the instrument or issuer; an exempt transaction depends on the circumstances of a particular transaction. Neither exemption licenses fraud, and neither automatically exempts every participating person from registration.

**49. A — Cancellation.** Cancellation is generally nonpunitive record cleanup for circumstances such as death, dissolution, or inability to locate a registrant who has ceased business. Revocation or suspension is disciplinary; injunction, conviction, and restitution are judicial or remedial concepts, not the described cleanup action.

**50. B — False official-approval implication and guarantee.** Registration does not represent merit approval, skill certification, or government insurance. Misstating registration and guaranteeing against loss are serious communication violations. Form ADV disclosure does not cure a false claim.

**51. B — Misleading waiver language is problematic.** An adviser may not use contract language to mislead clients into believing they waived nonwaivable statutory or fiduciary rights. Bold type, discretion, or wrap fees do not make a deceptive hedge clause enforceable. Contract analysis remains fact- and law-specific.

**52. B — Principal transaction.** Selling from the adviser’s own account places the adviser opposite the client as principal and creates a direct conflict. Applicable adviser law generally requires written conflict disclosure and client consent before completion. It is not an agency cross merely because both parties are clients, and fiduciary duties continue.

**53. B — Best total result reasonably available.** Best execution is broader than the lowest commission and considers execution price and quality, costs, speed, likelihood, financial responsibility, and other circumstances. An adviser need not guarantee the day’s lowest price, but must periodically and reasonably evaluate routing, including conflicts.

**54. A — Discretion.** Authority to choose securities or amounts without trade-by-trade consent is discretionary authority and can require written authorization and compliance with state or federal rules. Discretion does not automatically equal custody, though some authority can create separate custody questions. It is neither an exemption nor a guarantee.

**55. B — Qualified custodian and required safeguards.** Custody rules seek segregation, qualified custodians, client notice/statements, and surprise examination or other controls as applicable. Commingling, using client money for rent, or withholding statements exposes clients to misuse and violates basic fiduciary safeguards.

**56. B — Material nonpublic information.** An unannounced failed clinical trial is likely material; trading puts while aware of it raises insider-trading concerns depending on duty, source, and other facts. Portfolio-allocation and tax techniques do not address misuse of information.

**57. A — Churning or excessive trading.** Trading driven by compensation rather than reasonable client benefit can breach fiduciary duty and constitute excessive trading, especially where the adviser controls or influences activity. It is the opposite of passive management and is not cured by labeling the client qualified.

**58. B — Check prohibitions, exceptions, and conflict.** Adviser borrowing from a client is often prohibited except under narrow relationships or regulated-lender exceptions, and it presents a serious fiduciary conflict. Client willingness alone may not satisfy the rule or informed-consent standard; concealment worsens the issue.

**59. B — Execute incident response and assess duties.** A reasonable program contains the event, investigates scope, preserves evidence, assesses legal notification and reporting obligations, remediates, documents, and communicates appropriately. Waiting for theft, destroying logs, or exposing account numbers conflicts with safeguarding obligations.

**60. B — Follow vulnerable-adult protocols.** Abrupt, confusing transfers to a new acquaintance are red flags. The professional should document and escalate, use lawful trusted-contact and reporting/hold mechanisms where authorized, and avoid unsupported accusations. Protective statutes do not authorize taking ownership of assets.

## Practice-exam score analysis

- **Questions 1–9:** Domain I; 9 questions.
- **Questions 10–24:** Domain II; 15 questions.
- **Questions 25–42:** Domain III; 18 questions.
- **Questions 43–60:** Domain IV; 18 questions.
- **Total:** 60 questions and 60 rationales.

Do not convert a 60-question practice score directly into NASAA’s raw 92-of-130 criterion. This is a learning sample, not a psychometrically equated examination. Review misses by objective and reasoning cause; a correct guess belongs in the error log too.


---

# Bonus Practice Examination B (60 Questions)

### Questions 1–9 — Economic Factors and Business Information

1. A manufacturer reports that finished-goods inventories are rising unexpectedly while new orders and weekly production hours are falling. Which interpretation is most reasonable?
   A. Demand is accelerating, so the company should increase production immediately.
   B. Demand may be weakening, which is consistent with movement toward contraction.
   C. Inflation must be falling because inventories and prices always move together.
   D. The data prove that the economy has already reached a trough.

2. Which Federal Reserve action is most directly associated with restrictive monetary policy, all else equal?
   A. Purchasing Treasury securities in the open market
   B. Lowering the interest rate paid on reserve balances
   C. Lowering the discount rate
   D. Selling Treasury securities in the open market

3. A U.S. investor buys a euro-denominated stock. During the holding period, the stock gains 6% in euros, but the euro falls 8% against the U.S. dollar. Ignoring fees and taxes, what is the investor's approximate dollar return?
   A. -2.48%
   B. -2.00%
   C. 2.00%
   D. 14.48%

4. Under accrual accounting, a company delivers goods for $100,000 on credit. The goods had a $60,000 cost, and no cash is collected that day. What is the immediate effect, ignoring taxes?
   A. Revenue and operating cash flow both increase by $100,000.
   B. Revenue increases by $40,000, and inventory is unchanged.
   C. Revenue increases by $100,000, cost of goods sold increases by $60,000, and cash is unchanged.
   D. Nothing is recognized until the customer pays.

5. A business has current assets of $900,000, including $300,000 of inventory and $100,000 of prepaid expenses. Current liabilities are $400,000. Using the textbook's simplified formula, what is its quick ratio?
   A. 0.75
   B. 1.25
   C. 1.50
   D. 2.25

6. A project costs $10,000 today and will make one payment of $11,236 in two years. If the required annual return is 6%, what is the project's net present value, rounded to the nearest dollar?
   A. $0
   B. $600
   C. $1,236
   D. -$600

7. A portfolio returned 11%, the risk-free rate was 3%, and the portfolio's standard deviation was 16%. What was its Sharpe ratio?
   A. 0.19
   B. 0.69
   C. 1.33
   D. 0.50

8. Which risk is most directly reduced by holding a well-diversified portfolio of companies across many unrelated industries?
   A. A broad increase in real interest rates
   B. A nationwide recession
   C. The failure of one company's principal product
   D. An unexpected economy-wide inflation shock

9. A corporation is liquidated with too few assets to satisfy every claim. Assuming no special contractual complication, which claimant normally has priority over all the others listed?
   A. Preferred shareholder
   B. Senior unsecured bondholder
   C. Subordinated debenture holder
   D. Common shareholder

### Questions 10–24 — Investment Vehicle Characteristics

10. Which holding is potentially protected by federal deposit insurance when all coverage conditions and limits are satisfied?
   A. A demand deposit at an FDIC-insured bank
   B. Shares of a money market mutual fund purchased at a bank
   C. Commercial paper issued by the bank's holding company
   D. A bank-distributed variable annuity

11. A bond has $1,000 par value, a 5% annual coupon, and a market price of $920. What is its current yield?
   A. 5.00%
   B. 5.22%
   C. 5.43%
   D. 8.70%

12. A bond portfolio has modified duration of 7. If market yields rise by 0.75 percentage point, what price change does duration predict, before considering convexity?
   A. An increase of about 0.75%
   B. A decrease of about 0.93%
   C. An increase of about 5.25%
   D. A decrease of about 5.25%

13. Mortgage rates fall sharply, causing many homeowners to refinance. What is the most direct adverse effect on an investor in a mortgage-backed pass-through security?
   A. Extension risk increases because principal is returned later.
   B. Principal is returned sooner and may have to be reinvested at lower rates.
   C. The security automatically loses its guaranty.
   D. The coupon rate on every underlying mortgage increases.

14. A municipal bond yields 3.6% federally tax-exempt. For an investor with a 28% marginal federal income-tax rate, what taxable yield is equivalent, ignoring state tax and other differences?
   A. 5.00%
   B. 4.61%
   C. 2.59%
   D. 12.86%

15. A $1,000-par convertible bond is convertible at $50 per share. If the common stock trades at $56, what is the bond's conversion value?
   A. $560
   B. $1,000
   C. $1,120
   D. $1,200

16. An issuer omitted dividends on its cumulative preferred stock for two years. Which statement best describes the arrears?
   A. They disappear permanently because all preferred dividends are discretionary.
   B. They become secured claims equal to mortgage-bond interest.
   C. They must be paid immediately even if the board declares no dividend.
   D. They generally must be paid before the issuer may resume common dividends.

17. An open-end mutual fund has assets worth $420 million, liabilities of $20 million, and 20 million shares outstanding. It charges a 4% front-end sales load stated as a percentage of the public offering price. What are the NAV and public offering price per share, respectively?
   A. $21.00 and $21.88
   B. $20.00 and approximately $20.83
   C. $20.00 and $20.80
   D. $19.20 and $20.00

18. Which feature most clearly distinguishes a traditional closed-end fund from an open-end mutual fund after the initial offering?
   A. Closed-end shares trade in the market and may remain above or below NAV.
   B. Closed-end funds are required to maintain a constant $1 NAV.
   C. Closed-end shareholders redeem shares directly with the fund at the next NAV.
   D. Closed-end funds may invest only in fixed-income securities.

19. A client already owns eligible Class A shares in a fund family and plans another purchase. Counting the existing shares would reduce the front-end load on the new purchase. Which provision is most directly relevant?
   A. A contingent deferred sales charge
   B. Forward pricing
   C. Dollar-cost averaging
   D. A right of accumulation

20. An investor buys one put with a $60 strike price for a $4 premium. At expiration, the stock is $48. Ignoring transaction costs, what is the investor's profit on a standard 100-share contract?
   A. $400
   B. $1,200
   C. $800
   D. $5,600

21. Which statement correctly contrasts exchange-traded notes (ETNs) with exchange-traded funds (ETFs)?
   A. ETNs are equity interests in a segregated pool, while ETFs are unsecured debt.
   B. ETNs always have FDIC insurance, while ETFs do not.
   C. ETNs eliminate market and liquidity risk because their returns are formula-based.
   D. ETNs are unsecured issuer obligations, while ETF shareholders own interests in pooled assets.

22. An index begins at 100, gains 10% on Day 1, and loses 9.09% on Day 2, finishing approximately at 100. Ignoring expenses and tracking error, what happens to a daily +2x leveraged fund that also begins at 100?
   A. It finishes at approximately 98.18 because daily compounding is path dependent.
   B. It finishes at exactly 100 because the index's cumulative return is zero.
   C. It finishes at approximately 101.82 because leverage magnifies recovery.
   D. It finishes at 120 because only Day 1 matters.

23. Which annuity places investment performance risk most directly on the contract owner through separate-account choices?
   A. A traditional fixed annuity
   B. A variable annuity
   C. A fixed immediate annuity with life-only payments
   D. A conventional fixed indexed annuity

24. Which statement about a conventional physical commodity is most accurate?
   A. It generates contractual interest merely because it is held.
   B. It represents a residual claim on an operating company's earnings.
   C. Its return depends largely on price movement and carrying economics rather than contractual cash flow.
   D. It is insured against market loss when stored with a regulated dealer.

### Questions 25–42 — Client Investment Recommendations and Strategies

25. A client must pay a known tuition bill in 18 months and says she is willing to accept substantial volatility to earn more. Which consideration should dominate the recommendation for those tuition assets?
   A. The short time horizon and low capacity to recover from loss
   B. The client's stated willingness to speculate
   C. The recent performance of growth stocks
   D. The client's age standing alone

26. Under the Capital Asset Pricing Model, the risk-free rate is 3%, the expected market return is 9%, and a security's beta is 1.25. What return does CAPM require?
   A. 7.50%
   B. 9.00%
   C. 11.25%
   D. 10.50%

27. Which statement correctly distinguishes the Capital Market Line (CML) from the Security Market Line (SML)?
   A. The CML uses beta, while the SML uses standard deviation.
   B. The CML applies only to individual securities, while the SML applies only to efficient portfolios.
   C. The CML uses total risk for efficient portfolios, while the SML uses beta for securities or portfolios.
   D. Both lines use only company-specific risk.

28. Which version of the Efficient Market Hypothesis asserts that prices reflect all publicly available information but not necessarily material nonpublic information?
   A. Weak form
   B. Semi-strong form
   C. Strong form
   D. Random-walk accounting form

29. A portfolio's long-term target is 60% stocks and 40% bonds. It drifts to 68% stocks, and the adviser sells stocks to restore the policy target without making a market forecast. This is best described as
   A. strategic allocation with rebalancing.
   B. tactical asset allocation.
   C. sector rotation.
   D. high-frequency trading.

30. A client invests $300 at each of three prices: $30, $20, and $15. What is the client's average cost per share?
   A. $21.67
   B. $20.00
   C. $18.00
   D. $16.67

31. A client owns 100 shares purchased at $70 and buys one 65-strike put for $3. Ignoring costs other than the premium, what approximate minimum net value per share does the protective position establish at expiration?
   A. $65
   B. $62
   C. $67
   D. $70

32. A margin account holds securities worth $72,000 and has a $42,000 debit balance. What is the account's equity percentage?
   A. 30.00%
   B. 42.00%
   C. 58.33%
   D. 41.67%

33. In a taxable account, a client sells stock for $28,000. Adjusted basis is $19,000, and there are no relevant selling costs. What is the realized gain?
   A. $9,000
   B. $19,000
   C. $28,000
   D. $47,000

34. A client sells a security at a loss and buys a substantially identical security 20 days later. Under the general federal wash-sale rule, what normally happens?
   A. The loss becomes an ordinary loss with no limit.
   B. The replacement purchase is void.
   C. The loss is deferred and generally added to the replacement security's basis.
   D. The loss remains currently deductible because the repurchase occurred after the sale.

35. Which statement best describes a Roth IRA under general current federal principles?
   A. Contributions are always deductible, and all withdrawals are ordinary income.
   B. Contributions are after tax, and qualified distributions are generally federal income-tax free.
   C. The original owner must begin lifetime required minimum distributions at the same age as a traditional IRA owner.
   D. Direct contributions have no income eligibility restrictions.

36. Which plan places the primary investment risk on the employer because it promises a formula-based retirement benefit?
   A. A profit-sharing plan
   B. A 401(k) plan
   C. A nonqualified stock-option plan
   D. A defined benefit plan

37. A plan fiduciary relies on ERISA Section 404(c) for a participant-directed plan. Which duty generally remains with the fiduciary?
   A. Prudently selecting and monitoring the plan's investment menu
   B. Guaranteeing each participant against investment loss
   C. Selecting every participant's individual allocation
   D. Eliminating every investment with negative short-term performance

38. Parents want to save for college, retain control of unused assets, and avoid transferring unrestricted ownership to the child at the state's custodial termination age. Which account structure most directly fits that control objective, assuming its other features are suitable?
   A. A UTMA account
   B. An UGMA account
   C. A parent-owned 529 education savings account
   D. A joint account with rights of survivorship

39. Which statement about a UTMA or UGMA account is most accurate?
   A. The donor may revoke the gift whenever the child's plans change.
   B. The minor is the beneficial owner, while the custodian manages the property temporarily.
   C. The custodian becomes the permanent beneficial owner at termination.
   D. The account must be used exclusively for higher education.

40. Two people own property as tenants in common. One dies. In the absence of a controlling trust or other transfer arrangement, the decedent's share generally
   A. passes through the decedent's estate rather than automatically to the co-owner.
   B. passes automatically to the surviving co-owner by right of survivorship.
   C. escheats to the state immediately.
   D. becomes property of the account custodian.

41. A stock is quoted $39.80 bid and $40.10 ask. A customer enters an immediately executable market order to buy 200 shares in a stable market with sufficient displayed size. At which price would execution ordinarily be expected, before possible price improvement?
   A. $39.80
   B. $39.95
   C. $0.30
   D. $40.10

42. A manager had a +10% return before a large client contribution and a -10% return afterward. What is the time-weighted return for the two subperiods?
   A. 0%
   B. -10%
   C. -1%
   D. It cannot be calculated without the contribution amount.

### Questions 43–60 — Laws, Regulations, Guidelines, and Ethical Practices

43. A financial planner regularly provides individualized securities recommendations for a bundled planning fee. Which fact is most important in determining whether the planner is an investment adviser?
   A. Whether every recommendation results in a trade
   B. Whether the planner uses the title "investment adviser"
   C. Whether securities advice is provided as a business for direct or indirect compensation
   D. Whether the planner also discusses insurance

44. Under the federal AUM allocation buffer current as of August 11, 2026, which statement is generally correct for an adviser that is not exempt and has no special federal-registration route?
   A. A mid-sized adviser with $105 million of regulatory AUM may generally elect SEC registration if otherwise covered by the buffer.
   B. Every adviser below $110 million is prohibited from SEC registration in all circumstances.
   C. An SEC-registered adviser must withdraw immediately upon falling below $100 million.
   D. A state-registered adviser becomes SEC-registered automatically at $100 million without filing.

45. Which statement best describes a federal covered adviser's notice filing with a state?
   A. It converts the firm into a state-registered investment adviser.
   B. It may include SEC-filed documents, consent to service, and a fee, but it is not state IA registration.
   C. It eliminates the state's antifraud authority.
   D. It registers every associated IAR in the state automatically.

46. An out-of-state IAR has no place of business in State X and had five noninstitutional clients there during the preceding 12 months. Under the tested Uniform Securities Act de minimis model, which is most accurate?
   A. Registration is required because one client triggers it.
   B. Registration is never required for an out-of-state IAR.
   C. Registration is required because the maximum is four clients.
   D. The IAR may qualify for the de minimis exemption if all other conditions are satisfied.

47. A person employed by a broker-dealer only scans forms and performs ministerial data entry; the person neither solicits nor attempts to effect securities transactions. Under the Uniform Securities Act, this person is most likely
   A. not an agent because the functional sales test is not met.
   B. an agent because every broker-dealer employee is an agent.
   C. an investment adviser representative.
   D. an issuer.

48. A startup's employee offers the issuer's unregistered common stock to ordinary retail investors and receives a 4% commission on sales. Which statement is best?
   A. The employee can never be an agent when representing an issuer.
   B. The employee is excluded as long as the title "finder" is used.
   C. Transaction-based compensation and sales activity strongly indicate that agent registration must be analyzed.
   D. Registration of the security automatically registers the employee.

49. Which statement correctly distinguishes an exempt security from an exempt transaction under the Uniform Securities Act?
   A. An exempt transaction permanently exempts the security in all later sales.
   B. Antifraud rules do not apply to an exempt security.
   C. An exempt security concerns only the seller's registration.
   D. An exempt security carries its status while the exemption applies, whereas each later transaction needs its own transaction exemption analysis.

50. An issuer conducts an offering through a public website and wants to rely on Regulation D. Which route permits general solicitation if every purchaser is accredited and the issuer takes reasonable verification steps?
   A. The intrastate adviser exemption
   B. Rule 506(b)
   C. The Uniform Securities Act isolated nonissuer exemption
   D. Rule 506(c)

51. A security is properly offered in a Rule 506 transaction and is therefore federally covered. Which state power generally survives federal preemption?
   A. Requiring full state merit registration of the offering
   B. Rewriting the issuer's substantive offering terms
   C. Enforcing state antifraud law and requiring permitted notice filings and fees
   D. Declaring the offering approved merely because Form D was filed

52. Under the tested Uniform Securities Act private-transaction model, an issuer directs offers to 11 noninstitutional residents in the state during 12 months. Which statement is most accurate?
   A. The exemption applies because only purchasers, not offerees, count.
   B. The model exemption requiring no more than 10 such offerees is unavailable on these facts.
   C. The exemption applies whenever no commission is paid, regardless of offeree count.
   D. Rule 506(b)'s 35-purchaser concept automatically replaces the state limit.

53. A registered person tells a prospective client, "The Administrator approved my competence because my registration is effective." Why is this improper?
   A. Registration does not imply regulatory approval, endorsement, or a finding of competence.
   B. The statement is proper if the person's Form U4 is accurate.
   C. Only federal covered advisers may make the statement.
   D. The statement is proper if followed by a written disclaimer.

54. Under the NASAA state brochure model, when may an adviser deliver the brochure at the time the advisory contract is signed rather than at least 48 hours earlier?
   A. Only when the client is an institution
   B. Whenever the fee is asset based
   C. Only when the adviser has no discretion
   D. When the client may terminate without penalty within five business days

55. An adviser advertisement states, "Our strategy cannot lose money and will earn at least 8% each year." What is the primary problem?
   A. Percentages may never appear in advertisements.
   B. Advisers may guarantee performance if the claim is in writing.
   C. The statement improperly guarantees against loss and promises a return.
   D. The statement is permissible if most past years exceeded 8%.

56. Effective June 29, 2026, which natural-person test can independently qualify a client for a performance-fee contract under SEC Rule 205-3, assuming the rule's other conditions are met?
   A. Any positive net worth and $100,000 managed by the adviser
   B. At least $1.4 million managed by the adviser or net worth over $2.7 million under the rule's special calculation
   C. Annual income of $200,000 in only the current year
   D. Ownership of a primary residence worth $2.7 million regardless of debt

57. An advisory client authorizes an adviser to choose securities and quantities but gives no authority to withdraw funds except to pay trades. Which statement is most accurate?
   A. This is trading discretion, but trading discretion alone generally is not custody.
   B. Any discretion automatically constitutes custody.
   C. The authority is neither discretion nor custody.
   D. Custody exists only if the adviser physically holds certificates.

58. An adviser recommends that Client A buy shares directly from the adviser's proprietary account. This is best characterized as
   A. an ordinary agency trade with no special conflict.
   B. an agency cross because the adviser represents two clients.
   C. a principal transaction requiring specific conflict analysis, capacity disclosure, and transaction-specific consent under applicable law.
   D. a nonissuer transaction that eliminates fiduciary duties.

59. An adviser learns material nonpublic information about an issuer from a corporate officer who breached a duty by disclosing it. What is the most appropriate response?
   A. Trade before the information becomes stale.
   B. Refrain from trading or tipping and escalate under the firm's insider-trading controls.
   C. Trade only in discretionary accounts.
   D. Tell selected clients so everyone has an equal opportunity.

60. A client gives an IAR a broad written power to wire client funds to any bank account the IAR selects. Which conclusion is most accurate?
   A. The authority is merely time-and-price discretion.
   B. The authority is harmless if no wire has yet occurred.
   C. The authority eliminates the need for a qualified custodian.
   D. The authority to obtain possession of client funds generally creates custody and triggers custody safeguards.

## Answers and Rationales

1. **B.** Rising unplanned inventories alongside falling orders and production hours suggests goods are not selling as expected, a pattern consistent with weakening demand and possible contraction. A assumes the opposite of the evidence; C invents a necessary inflation relationship; D treats suggestive indicators as proof of a cycle trough.

2. **D.** An open-market sale withdraws reserves from the banking system and tends to put upward pressure on short-term rates, making policy more restrictive. Purchases and rate reductions in A–C are generally expansionary actions.

3. **A.** The exact combined return is $(1.06)(0.92)-1=-0.0248$, or -2.48%. B simply subtracts percentage returns and is only an approximation; C ignores currency loss; D incorrectly adds the absolute movements.

4. **C.** Accrual accounting recognizes the $100,000 sale when earned and the $60,000 matching expense when the goods are delivered; the unpaid sale creates a receivable, so cash is unchanged. A confuses revenue with cash flow, B records only gross profit as revenue, and D describes cash-basis timing.

5. **B.** Quick assets are $900,000-$300,000-$100,000=$500,000$; $500,000/$400,000=1.25$. C includes prepaid expenses, D is the current ratio, and A removes too many assets or reverses the logic.

6. **A.** The present value is $11,236/(1.06)^2=$10,000$, so NPV is zero. C is the undiscounted gain, while B and D do not discount the stated cash flow correctly.

7. **D.** Sharpe ratio equals $(11%-3%)/16%=0.50$. A divides the risk-free rate by volatility, B omits the risk-free deduction, and C reverses or otherwise misuses the inputs.

8. **C.** A single product failure is company-specific, or unsystematic, risk and can be reduced through diversification. Interest-rate, recession, and inflation shocks in A, B, and D are broad systematic risks that diversification cannot eliminate.

9. **B.** Senior unsecured debt ranks ahead of subordinated debt and both preferred and common equity. A and D are equity claims; C is contractually junior to senior unsecured debt.

10. **A.** An eligible demand deposit at an FDIC-insured bank may receive deposit insurance within applicable limits and ownership rules. A money market mutual fund, commercial paper, and variable annuity are investments or securities, not insured deposits merely because a bank sells or affiliates with them.

11. **C.** Annual coupon is $1,000\times5%=$50$; current yield is $50/$920=5.43%$. A is the coupon rate, B uses an incorrect denominator, and D resembles the discount percentage rather than current yield.

12. **D.** The duration estimate is $-7\times0.0075=-0.0525$, or about -5.25%. A ignores duration and sign, B divides rather than multiplies, and C has the wrong direction because bond prices generally fall as yields rise.

13. **B.** Falling mortgage rates encourage refinancing, returning principal early and forcing reinvestment at lower yields—prepayment and reinvestment risk. A describes what commonly happens when rates rise; C and D do not follow from refinancing.

14. **A.** Tax-equivalent yield is $3.6%/(1-0.28)=5.00%$. C calculates an after-tax taxable yield instead; B and D arise from incorrect treatment of the tax rate.

15. **C.** The conversion ratio is $1,000/$50=20$ shares; $20\times$56=$1,120$. B ignores the stock's value above the conversion price, while A and D use incorrect ratios.

16. **D.** Cumulative preferred dividends omitted in prior periods accrue and ordinarily must be satisfied before common dividends resume. They are not secured contractual interest (B), are not automatically erased (A), and need not be paid immediately absent a declaration or triggering term (C).

17. **B.** NAV is $(420-20)/20=$20$ per share. Because the load is a percentage of POP, $POP=$20/(1-0.04)\approx$20.83$. C incorrectly adds 4% of NAV; A and D misstate NAV.

18. **A.** Closed-end shares typically trade between investors at market prices that can persist at premiums or discounts to NAV. B describes neither the general closed-end structure nor a requirement; C describes open-end redemption; D invents an asset restriction.

19. **D.** A right of accumulation counts eligible existing holdings toward a Class A sales-load breakpoint. A is a redemption charge, B determines which NAV applies, and C is a periodic investment technique.

20. **C.** Put intrinsic value is $60-$48=$12$ per share; subtract the $4 premium for an $8 profit per share, or $800 for 100 shares. B ignores the premium, A treats premium as profit, and D is unrelated to the payoff.

21. **D.** An ETN is the issuer's unsecured senior debt linked to an index or formula, so its holder bears issuer credit risk. An ETF shareholder owns an interest in a pooled portfolio. A reverses the structures, B invents deposit insurance, and C ignores market, liquidity, formula, and issuer risks.

22. **A.** The leveraged fund rises 20% to 120, then loses about 18.18% of 120, ending near 98.18. B ignores daily resetting, C gets the direction of volatility drag wrong, and D ignores the second day's return.

23. **B.** A variable annuity uses separate-account investment options whose performance, after fees, changes contract value; the owner bears market risk. Fixed and conventional fixed-indexed designs in A, C, and D place the direct investment promise with the insurer, subject to contract terms and insurer credit.

24. **C.** A physical commodity produces no earnings, dividends, or contractual interest merely from being held; return depends on price, storage and financing economics, and the investment vehicle. A and B assign it bond or equity cash flows, while D invents market-loss insurance.

25. **A.** A mandatory payment in 18 months leaves little time to recover from a market decline, so capacity for loss and liquidity outweigh an abstract willingness to take risk. B confuses willingness with capacity, C chases performance, and D treats age as decisive without analyzing the goal.

26. **D.** CAPM gives $3%+1.25(9%-3%)=10.50%$. A omits part of the beta adjustment, B simply uses the market return, and C multiplies beta by the full market return rather than the market risk premium.

27. **C.** The CML plots expected return against total risk, measured by standard deviation, for efficient portfolios; the SML plots required return against beta and can evaluate securities or portfolios. A and B reverse these features, and D substitutes diversifiable risk for the models' risk measures.

28. **B.** Semi-strong efficiency says prices reflect all publicly available information. Weak form in A covers past price and volume data; strong form in C includes public and private information; D is not a standard EMH form.

29. **A.** Returning a drifted portfolio to a long-term policy target without a forecast is strategic allocation with rebalancing. Tactical allocation and sector rotation in B and C deliberately change exposures based on outlook; HFT in D concerns rapid automated trading.

30. **B.** The purchases acquire 10, 15, and 20 shares, respectively—45 shares for $900—so average cost is $900/45=$20$. A is the arithmetic average of the three prices, not cost per share; C and D use incorrect share totals.

31. **B.** At expiration the put permits sale at $65, but the $3 premium reduces the net floor to approximately $62 per share. A ignores the premium, C adds rather than subtracts it, and D assumes the original purchase price is guaranteed.

32. **D.** Equity is $72,000-$42,000=$30,000$; equity percentage is $30,000/$72,000=41.67%$. A states equity in thousands as a percentage, B roughly uses the loan balance, and C is the debit balance divided by market value.

33. **A.** Realized gain is amount realized minus adjusted basis: $28,000-$19,000=$9,000$. B is basis, C is proceeds, and D adds rather than subtracts the two amounts.

34. **C.** A purchase 20 days after the loss sale falls within the period from 30 days before through 30 days after the sale. The loss is generally deferred, added to replacement basis, and accompanied by a holding-period adjustment. A, B, and D misstate the consequence or timing.

35. **B.** Roth IRA contributions are made after tax, and qualified distributions are generally federal income-tax free. A describes neither Roth contribution nor distribution treatment; C ignores that original Roth IRA owners generally have no lifetime RMDs under current law; D ignores income limits on direct contributions.

36. **D.** A defined benefit plan promises a formula-based benefit, leaving the employer or plan sponsor with primary actuarial and investment risk. The arrangements in A and B generally make the participant's account value depend on contributions and performance, and C is not a formula-based pension plan.

37. **A.** Section 404(c) may provide limited relief for losses resulting from a participant's control, but it does not erase the fiduciary's duty to prudently select and monitor the menu, fees, and structure. B creates a guarantee, C negates participant direction, and D replaces prudent process with a short-term-results test.

38. **C.** A parent-owned 529 generally lets the owner control investments, distributions, and eligible beneficiary changes, avoiding mandatory transfer of unrestricted control at a custodial termination age. UTMA and UGMA gifts in A and B irrevocably belong to the minor; D is not a purpose-built education account and does not solve the stated planning problem.

39. **B.** In a UTMA or UGMA, the gift is irrevocable and beneficially owned by the minor, while the custodian manages it until termination under state law. A contradicts irrevocability, C confuses management with ownership, and D invents an education-only restriction.

40. **A.** Tenancy in common has no inherent right of survivorship, so the deceased owner's share normally passes under a will, trust, or intestacy. B describes JTWROS, while C and D are unsupported by the ownership form.

41. **D.** A buyer demanding immediate execution ordinarily pays near the ask, here $40.10, assuming sufficient size and a stable quote. A is where an immediate seller trades, B is merely the midpoint, and C is the spread rather than a share price.

42. **C.** Time-weighted return geometrically links subperiods: $(1.10)(0.90)-1=-1%$. A arithmetically averages returns, B reports only the second period, and D is wrong because TWR neutralizes the amount and timing of external cash flows.

43. **C.** The central adviser test asks whether securities advice or analysis is provided as a business for compensation, direct or indirect. A trade need not result, titles do not control (B), and discussing another product (D) does not defeat adviser status.

44. **A.** As of August 11, 2026, the Rule 203A-1 buffer generally permits an otherwise eligible mid-sized adviser in the $100 million-to-below-$110 million band to elect SEC registration. B ignores exceptions and the buffer, C ignores the below-$90 million withdrawal buffer and transition process, and D ignores required filings.

45. **B.** A state notice filing for a federal covered adviser can consist of SEC-filed material, consent to service, and a fee; it does not turn the firm into a state-registered IA. States retain antifraud authority (contrary to C), and IAR registration is separate (contrary to D); A collapses notice filing into registration.

46. **D.** Under the tested model, an out-of-state IA or IAR with no in-state place of business may qualify with no more than five noninstitutional clients during the preceding 12 months, if all other conditions are met. A and C use the wrong threshold, while B wrongly makes the exemption unlimited.

47. **A.** Agent status is functional: the individual must represent a broker-dealer or issuer in effecting or attempting to effect securities transactions. Purely clerical or ministerial activity does not meet that test. B ignores function, and C and D are different regulated categories.
